Verified in `make test-env-matrix`: the rule clamps (`env_flash_boost(0) == 0`, `env_flash_boost(1000) <= 300`, saturates above 1000), a strike raises bleed and exposure over the same storm, the bleeds whiten, and the strike measurably lands on a dark surface through the production `env_matrix_apply_surface` transform. Still NOT verified: the lobby is GL-bound and no SDL2 headers exist in this sandbox, so the `env_in.flash = g_sky_weather.flash` wiring and the live look are reviewed, not built.
- **feat(lighting): one unified environment matrix, resolved in PARENA — the "too dark everywhere" fix.** Founder real-time: "we have cool weather effects but it doesnt work with the ambient lighting like it always looks too dark everywhere we have some lighting tech down but we need more of a theory of all the lighting needs to talk to each other." The diagnosis was exact: weather (`sky_weather_light_state`), the day/night preset, the ambient slider (`ambient_rules.prn`) and the emitters each adjusted the scene independently, and every one of them could only subtract — a stormy night at slider 0 multiplied ambient down to near-black walls because nothing tracked how much light the world actually gets. New `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/lighting_rules.prn` (generated by the new `scripts/gen_lighting_rules.sh` into `packages/simulation/lighting_rules.c`) resolves the atmosphere into six i32-permille values — occlusion (capped at 0.78), light bleed, ambient floor, exposure, emissive scale, emitter range/spread — and everything reads those instead of deriving its own. New thin host layer `packages/render/env_matrix.{h,c}` only converts units and applies: `env_matrix_apply` dims ambient gently (18% at worst) and sun/moon fully, `env_matrix_publish` hands the floor and the scattered sky-colour bleed to `retro_lighting`, and `env_matrix_apply_surface` (one function, one translation unit, no GL) adds the bleed and lifts the combined ambient term to the floor with a uniform gain (floor ÷ brightest channel) — hue-preserving, not a per-channel clamp, so walls do not wash flat. `retro_lighting_eval_surface_rgb` calls that exact function for every surface in the game; with nothing published it is a bit-for-bit no-op, so every existing caller and test is unchanged. Interiors keep only 35% of the occlusion and get a higher floor and no open-sky bleed. Emitters now trade reach for spread: fog shortens `FLASHLIGHT_RANGE` and widens `FLASHLIGHT_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` through one derived number, applied to the beam mesh, the per-face lit cone and the litbox spotlight uniforms so they cannot disagree; the enemy outline scales its emissive by the same scene-brightness inverse (bright noon tucks it down to 0.75, a dark scene pushes it up). Deliberately NOT described as Rayleigh/Mie scattering — it models the three things that were actually broken, not a physical sky this renderer cannot support. Costs one CPU evaluation per frame from values both sides already broadcast (no server work, no new wire fields, no per-entity state) and is a pure function, so every client computes the identical matrix bit for bit — the property that keeps it cheap at MMO concurrence instead of syncing lighting. Verified: `make test-env-matrix` (`-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, ASan+UBSan, links no GL) — PARENA purity/clamps, clear-weather no-op, the stormy-night regression through the exact production function (shaded-wall luminance 0.209 → 0.358, floor 0.300, occlusion 0.78, bleed 0.32), publish→surface wiring and its inverse, slider monotonicity under the worst weather, bit-identical determinism, and the emitter trade-off across the whole visibility range. NOT verified here: `retro_lighting` is GL-bound and this sandbox has no SDL2 headers, so `make test-ambient-boost` (recipe updated for the new link dependency) and the live look could not be built or seen; `apps2/`'s duplicate tree untouched.
- **feat(weapons): the assault rifle's underbarrel grenade — right click, E, and the controller's left trigger.** Founder real-time: "add an E for 3 the assault rifle ... the traditional right click thing where it fires a grenade like if its an m4 or whatever with the grenade powerup attached have e and right click both trigger it and ensure we have left trigger affordances for controller ... it should do a projectile and it should be fast like soldier 76 rocket and it should be a bit of like an arch down like its not so fast that its instant like a rail gun ... cool fireball shaders and explosions ... when it hits stuff it should cause an explosion and ensure it behaves with our destructable geometry." `WPN_AR`'s existing Frag Toss (a 0.7x lob) is replaced by a real rifle grenade: `AR_GRENADE_SPEED_MULT` 2.2x the 4.0 units/tick base (~9 units/tick, fast enough to read as a rocket, still a flight you watch), new `Projectile.gravity` (`AR_GRENADE_GRAVITY` 0.22 units/tick²) integrated in `update_projectiles` so it arcs instead of flying dead straight, damage 75, splash 4.5, 260-tick cooldown. New `BTN_ABILITY_2` (bit 2048) is a genuinely separate input from E, not a duplicate: right mouse and controller LEFT trigger (fire is the right trigger; LB was already the ability) set it — separate because the sniper's zoom IS right mouse, and folding RMB into `BTN_ABILITY_1` would spend the storm charges every time someone scoped (asserted in the new test). `Projectile.weapon` now carries the firing weapon into `explode_splash` instead of a hardcoded `WPN_MISSILE`, so the blast hook carves destructible brick with AR's own x2 multiplier rather than a rocket's x5 — a grenade opens a hole where a missile deletes the wall. Presentation: a real GLSL fireball (same hand-loaded GL 2.0 path `gl_shader.h` gives the flashlight beam, so it survives the mingw build) draws one camera-facing billboard per detonation — layered value noise over a sphere normal, white-hot core cooling to orange then smoke, plus a shockwave ring racing ahead of the flame — blended additively, with a second shell, a 12-ember spray and a fading trail on the round itself, all driven by `g_fx_explosion_hook` from `explode_splash` (NULL on the server and in tests, so nothing but pixels changes). No shader support degrades to an additive glow quad instead of nothing. NOT a port of Bedrock Racers' fire: BR's flames are JS/WebGL2 particle data in WOTAN and this renderer is fixed-function GL 1.x, so the shader is written for this pipeline rather than pretending a copy. Verified: `make test-ar-grenade` (new, 30 assertions through the real `update_weapons` — grenade numbers, upward velocity, faster-than-missile speed, cooldown, both input bits against one cooldown, sniper zoom does not spend charges, non-AR ignores the bit, missile keeps gravity 0 and its own id), `make test-hammer` and `make test-brick-world` still pass after the signature changes, and the FX block type-checks standalone under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. NOT verified: no live match, no GPU (no display/SDL2 here), so the shader and the glow's actual look are unproven, and net clients still render no projectiles at all (pre-existing: projectiles are not sent over the wire, so a grenade in a networked match is server-side only until that is built).
- **feat(lobby): NOCK is an APPS entry -- it starts the server and opens the editor.** The local map tools used to need another terminal (`make local-nock`) or a `.bat` before the browser editor was reachable; the APPS page now has a `NOCK` tile (`APP_NOCK`) that spawns the PARENA server itself and opens `http://127.0.0.1:8787/` in the system browser. Dev tree: `bin/local_nock_parena` with `--root <checkout>` (so saves land in `levels/` and get committed in this repo); packaged: `bundled/dw_nock{,.exe}` with `bundled/levels` + `bundled/tools/nock_editor.html`. The port is probed with a 100 ms non-blocking connect, so an already-running server (previous click, or the user's own `make local-nock`) is reused instead of double-started, and the tab opens only once the port answers (bounded 2 s) rather than racing the browser into a connection error. The server is spawned detached, so the editor keeps working after quitting the lobby; a failure is reported in the lobby footer instead of passing silently. The server grew `--port/--root/--repo/--levels/--editor` and `--help` for exactly this (flags win over `LOCAL_NOCK_*`, which still work), because a child's command line is one call on Windows while an environment block is not. Verified: both new functions compile clean standalone under `-std=c99 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` (that check caught two real defects first -- a `/*` inside a block comment and `-Wformat-truncation` on the path buffers, both fixed), `make test-local-nock-parena` 16/16, and a real `--root` run serving the level list, the editor page, a save that wrote `levels/cli_smoke.json` and committed it in a throwaway repo. Not verified from the real lobby here (no SDL2/display on this box) and Windows is not bundled yet.
- **fix(release): `dw_gui.exe` link failed — `core/draft.c` missing from the hand-copied source list.** Ninth occurrence of the drift class this file's own release.yml comments already document: DEADWEIGHT's `scripts/build.sh` `GUI_SRC` gained `core/draft.c` (S510 `DW_C_DRAFT_RESUME`, `dw_draft_deck_valid` / `dw_random_deck`) and neither copy in `release.yml` -- the Windows `dw_gui.exe` mingw line nor the Linux `dw_gui` line -- had it, so the link failed with `undefined reference to dw_random_deck` (from `core/client.c`) and `dw_draft_deck_valid` (from `core/iduna.c`). Both lists now match `GUI_SRC` exactly, plus `core/runtime/parena_runtime.c` (the `arena_alloc` fix from the earlier outage). Verified without a mingw/SDL2 toolchain here: the yml lists were diffed against `GUI_SRC` (no missing files, every path exists in DEADWEIGHT), and `core/draft.c` compiles clean under `-std=c99 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` with `nm` confirming both symbols. Next CI run is the real proof.
- **feat(paridad): the local NOCK server now runs in PARENA.** `tools/local_nock.prn` + the previously missing `PARENA/stdlib/net/http_server.prn` emit and serve real HTTP: `make local-nock-parena` builds the generated C with `gcc -std=c99 -pedantic -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and runs it on the same API (level list/read/save with the real git commit, session create/events/draft, editor page). Verified live over loopback: `PUT /api/levels/smoke` wrote the file and committed `level(smoke) from local NOCK` in a throwaway repo, `POST` session returned 32 hex, events posted and read back, draft pushed/fetched, `/` served the editor, unknown paths 404. Host boundary (`tools/local_nock_host.c`/`.h`, prototypes included via `-include` like PARENA's own `ci_status.h`) is only the arena/env, file and directory access, and the session strings that outlive one request. Getting it to build needed four real emitter fixes (variadic arithmetic, `set!` on a top-level def, `println` in the runtime, and `&` on an already-pointer match payload that emitted `void **`), plus a real runtime fix in `tcp_read_impl` (a bodyless request was read until EOF, so every GET hung). Parity landed the same day: `?since` filtering, source/kind validation, draft revisions and the saved-level fallback are all in PARENA now, and both runners pass the same 16 checks (`make test-local-nock`, `make test-local-nock-parena`, the latter driving the binary through `tools/test_local_nock.py --binary`). Sessions are hardened on both: the id segment must be the live room (a stale client is 404, never a write into the current one), a level name must be slug-shaped before any path is built (the traversal defence), bodies over 4 MiB are 413 (and the Python runner now drains an oversized upload before answering, so the client reads the status instead of dying on a broken pipe), wrong methods are 405, unknown sources/kinds are 400, the event log is capped at 2000 entries with the oldest dropped, and the listing counts the `walls` array rather than trusting the file. Two more real fixes came out of it: `set!` on a String-typed top-level def went through the pointer branch (`*(name) = ...`) instead of the global, and `net/http_server`'s `trim` only stripped spaces/tabs, so a `git rev-parse` newline made the save response invalid JSON. Remaining, named: the PARENA runner is one room per process and single-threaded (`wait` is answered immediately; the test verifies poll-again instead), and it validates that a level is an object with a walls array but not each wall's fields. Not driven from the lobby or a browser here (no SDL2/display, no Chrome).
- **feat(local-nock): the map editor runs locally, browser and game in one live session.** `make local-nock` (`python3 tools/local_nock.py`) serves `tools/nock_editor.html` and the same edit-session API the native client already speaks (`SHANKPIT_EDIT_SESSIONS_URL`, IDUNA `shankpit_edit_sessions.go` shape): level list/read/save, session create, events, working-draft push/fetch. The browser pushes the working level and the game reloads it; the game's box/spawner/avatar events appear in the browser. Saves land in `levels/<name>.json` and are committed (`level(<name>): N walls from local NOCK`, never pushed), with `levels/starter.json` as the first map. `make test-local-nock` (12 checks) covers the HTTP contract including the git commit, invalid levels, long-poll wakeup and 404s. Gaps named: one live room per run, and the event log replays whole instead of honouring `?since` (applied events are idempotent). PARENA port started: `PARENA/stdlib/net/http_server.prn` (request parsing + response serialisation, emits clean) plus `tools/local_nock.prn`, which parses but is blocked on four verified VS0 gaps (reference params / `match` scrutinees naming an imported defstruct, pointer-typed returns, `set!` on a top-level def), so the Python server stays the runner for now.
- **feat(edit-map): F6 saves a real level JSON, no IDUNA needed.** EDIT MAP's F6 writes the whole working level (source plus every edit) to `var/maps/<name>.json` through the new `level_boxes_save_to_file` (`packages/world/level_boxes.h`), which emits every field the loader parses -- walls/materials, spawners, buggy spawns, lab stations, doors (button subscriptions + script paths included), buttons, nav nodes, characters, exits, zones, edu widgets, brick damage, enclosed/floor tint/next level. Load it back with `--edit-map var/maps/<name>.json` or `--level`. F5's op log stays the live-NOCK streaming format; a successful F6 deletes the stale `.edits.json` so a replay can't apply the same edits twice. `level_boxes_parse_string` now honours `\\ \" \n \t \r` escapes (real escaped JSON, and it fixes Windows door script paths coming back doubled). `make test-level-save`, plus a round-trip of the checked-in `var/lab/lab.json`, the story-doors demo and every `assets/tyler_levels/*.json` export. Not driven in the live client here (no SDL2/display on this box); writer + loader round-trip is what's tested.
- **feat(tools): `leveltool`, headless level JSON utility.** `info` (summary of a map), `check` (load -> save -> load and fail if any parsed field changed shape, plus a stable-writer byte check), `save` (normalize/re-emit) and `new` (an empty level, same defaults as EDIT MAP's new-level path). `map` prints a top-down ASCII view (`#` wall, `S` spawner, `C` character, `E` exit, `z` zone, `D` door, `B` button) so a layout can be eyeballed with no display; `--cell` zooms to a fixed scale (centred on `--center`, else spawner 0) and `--min-height` hides short geometry (a 500-unit LAB floor slab otherwise squashes the plan into a few cells). Walls rasterise their footprint outline, so a long wall crossing the window draws along its whole length. `make leveltool`, `make test-leveltool`, and `make check-levels` which runs `check` over `var/lab/lab.json`, the story-doors demo, `var/zombie/nextown_zombies.json` and every `assets/tyler_levels/*.json`. `--level var/maps/<name>.json` also already plays a locally saved map (both sides must pass the same path).

## 2026-10-16

- **fix(brick): the XPBD rigid-body debris is replaced by a cheap fake** (founder: it was slow, inconsistent, glass worse; "revert, fake it, no piling"). Shot-out cells shed the old flakes again. Cut-off geometry (glass disc, wall section) falls as one chunk with gravity + a seeded tumble, one level probe per chunk per tick, one small bounce, lies flat on the floor or whatever it lands on, then lingers 5 s. Max 32 chunks, no solver/pairs/piling; the dedicated server no longer simulates debris at all. Mass/density (NOCK material) kept as data. Headless tests only; live feel not verified.
- **feat(zombies): pheromone thrower is a real grenade.** `G` lobs a 0.8 kg canister (`packages/simulation/pheromone_grenade.h`: impulse/mass launch speed, ballistic arc, mass-dependent bounce, wall bounce, Coulomb roll to rest, inherits the thrower's velocity). The marker zombies chase rides the canister, so they track it in the air and through each bounce; the thud on landing startles zombies in range. Visuals: tumbling glowing canister with a streak, pulsing ground rings + scent wisps + smell-radius ring when down, G-key READY/cooldown HUD, red diamonds over starving zombies. `make test-pheromone-grenade`. Not seen in the live game.
- **feat(zombies): hunger infighting.** A zombie whose hunger >= 0.70 with the hero out of sense smells the nearest zombie / citizen / Man (up to 135 units), hunts it and bites (16 dmg, feeds it); a bitten zombie fights back; The Men melee zombies within 14 units. Dead victims are permanent corpses. Tuning (rates, radii) is a first pass.


- feat(brick): **physical broken brick + falling disconnected geometry.** Shot-out cells throw mass-carrying shards (brick ~15 kg, density x volume) and any group of cells cut off from the ground/static solids falls as a rigid chunk (shoot a circle out of glass and the disc drops, with a seeded tumble), all on GOLDEN BAND's `grb` XPBD solver via new `packages/simulation/brick_bodies.{h,c}` (shards pile as cubes, slide down ramps via `obb.h`). `brick_fracture.h`: bounded flood-fill detection `bf_detect_detached` (found and fixed a real bug in its own tests: a flood that met an earlier flood's cells wrongly treated itself as cut off), chunk id on the brick wire pad byte, shard queue, per-parent density. Mass configurable per NOCK material (`density` kg/m^3 in `level_boxes.h`; IDUNA migration + Materials screen field). Server and lobby tick the bodies; lobby draws them (glass translucent). New `make test-brick-bodies` / `test-brick-density`; `test-brick-fracture` and `test-brick-world` extended. Bodies are per-client cosmetic (not synced), do not hit players; live visual check not done (scripted input unreliable), headless tests only. README updated.

- fix(windows): real in-phone PELLEGRINO browser on Windows via `scripts/build_pgb_windows.sh` (mingw; stub fallback, cacert.pem shipped). Untested on Windows (CI-built).

- feat(lobby): **GFD phone app is on the cross-game chat bus** (`packages/gfd_chat/gfd_chat.h`, tested by `gfd_chat_test.c`). Messages typed in the phone's GFD terminal post to IDUNA `/api/v1/chat/messages` (channel `shankpit`) under the player's IDUNA display name and are polled back every 3s, so every SHANKPIT GFD phone sees them regardless of game/mode, plus the GFD MUD's `global` chat (shown as `[GFD] name`). Requires signing in via the phone's IDUNA app first (the token is the credential). Verified end to end with two lobby clients plus a simulated MUD post against a fake chat-bus server; NOT yet against the real IDUNA (needs the IDUNA `shankpit` channel deployed). Windows: stubbed no-op. README updated.
- **Phone IDUNA app is live in-game (2026-10-16):** the SHANKPHONE IDUNA tile (zombies/lab/story) now runs the exact same loopback SSO flow as the legacy menu's LOGIN IDUNA button (system browser -> IDUNA hosted login -> 127.0.0.1 token return), polled every frame during play; shows `CODE: BROWSER` while pending, `LINKED: <name>` on success, an error on timeout. Replaces the "use IDUNA.GAME" stub. Pending state verified live under Xvfb; the full token return is untested here (no IDUNA reachable).
- **feat(phone): PELLEGRINO in-phone browser now builds and runs.** `make lobby` links `packages/pellegrino_browser_embed` (mbedTLS + monocypher + embed objects, `PGB_OBJS`, kept out of `LOBBY_SRC`). Verified live under Xvfb `--zombies`: page renders in the phone panel, URL typing navigates, Esc returns to the home grid. Open: mouse, in-game `h3://` check, Windows/CI wiring. README updated.

## 2026-10-16

- fix(brick-fracture): destruction no longer stops in busy/complex levels. The shared 900-piece
  collision budget used to roll every further shot back silently once enough walls were holed
  (also: 160 removed cells/parent and a 4096-record table). `bf_rebuild` now falls back to
  progressively coarser blocks instead of failing, removed-per-parent is 1024, the record table is
  16384 with compaction of merely-cracked cells when full. Test updated (`brick_fracture_test.c`).

## 2026-10-15

- **feat(edu): programmable NOCK level widgets bound to EduVM** (card #660; founder-pasted plan for "interactive widgets inside the level editor so players can program the world"). New `edu_widgets` level array (`entity_id`, `machine_type` gate/bridge/portal/crate/plate, `initial_state`, `memory_address`, `x/y/z`, `radius`, `box_index`) parsed by `level_boxes.h`; EduVM gains `set_state(id,v)`, `set_reg(reg,v)`, `read_sensor(reg)`, `read_state(id)` over a 16-slot widget table (register clashes rejected, plates host-written only, unwired reads 0, world reset restores authored states). Lobby binds widgets on level load, plates sense players, `box_index` widgets move their wall like a story door, E near a widget opens the Orb on a seeded "Gate Sequencer" slot. New level `examples/programmable/TUTORIAL_PROGRAMMABLE.json`. Verified: `make test-edu-widgets`, existing eduvm/array/zone/baked-level tests, `make server`, stubbed-lobby harness against the real VM. NOT run in a live lobby (no SDL2/GLU); no NOCK editor/IDUNA store support for `edu_widgets` yet. Adapted from the paste: its `level_loader.h` doesn't exist (schema lives in `level_boxes.h`), and ids are strings while registers are ints, so `read_sensor` takes a register and `read_state` takes an id.
- **net:** both sprays now show on other players' screens (card #659). The registry-PNG spray (T) was a purely client-side decal; new `PACKET_SPRAY_DECAL` (16): client -> server -> every other welcomed client in the sender's scene (server validates finiteness/range/normal/spray id, ~8/s per sender, stamps its own scene id). The paint can already rode `NetPlayer` (weapon, colour in `ammo`, trigger in `is_shooting`), so remote players' paint was drawn from snapshots; added a ~150ms trigger latch so a remote spray doesn't stutter between snapshots. Verified: `make test-spray-decal` (wire layout) and a real two-client UDP run against `bin/shank_server` (B receives A's decal; A gets no echo; NaN / zero-normal dropped). Not run in a live lobby. Limits: decals placed before you joined don't replay; a remote spray id you haven't seen fetches its PNG on first draw (blocking, once per id).
- **feat(heli): flight-sim chase camera + J/L bank keys** (founder: "camera for the helicopter ... too low you cant really see the helicopter ... farther back and looking down a little more ... Q and E should also map to J and L ... camera anchored to the rotation of the tail"). Camera is now a rigid boom off the tail: 32 units back, 13 up, heading taken straight from the airframe yaw (no positional lag, so a right turn swings the view right), pitch derived from the boom so the heli stays framed; only height is eased. J = bank left (with Q), L = bank right (with E) while in a helicopter. `main.c` not linked on this box (no SDL2/GLU) and NOT feel-tested; `HELI_CAM_DIST/HEIGHT` are first guesses.
- **feat(weapons): spray can, weapon 9** (founder: "implement a spray can in SHANKPIT as weapon 9; [] toggles through the paint colors ... cone projection like the flashlight ... paint thickness ... hold the paint on a spot it should drip"). Key `9` equips `WPN_SPRAYCAN` (`MAX_WEAPONS` 9->10); hold fire to spray, `[` / `]` cycle cyan, yellow, magenta, white, black, grey, tan (rising-edge in `update_weapons`, colour index in `ammo[WPN_SPRAYCAN]` so it already rides `NetPlayer.ammo` -- no wire change; new `BTN_PAINT_NEXT/PREV` bits). Engine `packages/world/spraypaint.h` (GL-free): 6 rays per ~30 Hz tick inside a 9 degree cone, deposits merge into one dab per spot with thickness falling off with distance (close spray ~16x thicker per spot than from 10 units), opacity builds with thickness, a dab past thickness 1.0 on a wall grows a drip that draws paint out of it, runs down, stops above the floor / runs dry; floors pool, no drip. Drawn as soft blobs + drip strips + bead, plus a faint tinted cone from the nozzle, HUD swatch + name, can model in the paint colour. Paint is observed per player from synced `is_shooting`/weapon/colour like bullet holes, so others' paint shows; destroyed bricks take their paint with them. Verified: `make test-spraypaint` (13 headless checks), weapon-logic harness (colour wrap both ways, edge not level, reload doesn't touch colour), `main.c` `-fsyntax-only` clean of new warnings. NOT linked or run in a live match on this box (no SDL2/EDITOR.GAME sibling) -- look, drip speed and deposit rates (`SPRAY_*` constants) are untuned first guesses. Not built: paint for late joiners, sideways drip wander, hiss sound, scroll-wheel/gamepad colour cycle.
- **fix(net): blade-dash rubber-banding under reconciliation** (founder: "cant jump quite as high with the blade dash as i can in single player ... slight rubber banding"). `client_reconcile_local_player` replays acked-seq+1..latest from the server's state at the ack, but kept the client's LIVE `dash_timer`/`dash_v*`/`ability_cooldown`, so the replayed dash ended several ticks early (or never re-launched) and the replay landed lower than the original prediction -> a downward correction on every snapshot mid-dash. Now the client remembers the cmd seq that started the predicted dash (+ its launch velocity) and rebuilds the dash as of the ack tick before replaying. Headless harness (server vs lagged-prediction client, 5-tick lag, jump+dash): max reconcile error 27.7 -> 0.000 units, same peak height. `main.c` not linked on this box (no SDL2/EDITOR.GAME sibling) and NOT feel-tested live.
- **feat(physics): wall ride on ctrl-crouch** (kanban #648, founder: "wall ride just like lucio ... except momentum with shankpit ... we dont want to change ANYTHING about how that physics currently works"). Airborne + crouch held + touching a wall face magnets you to it: gravity cancelled, momentum along the wall kept (only the existing 1% `SLIDE_FRICTION` drag, so it bleeds off exactly like a ground slide), velocity into the wall cancelled, gentle pull to hold contact; release ctrl or run out of speed and you fall normally. Purely additive -- `phys_wall_ride_probe`/`phys_wall_ride_tick` in `physics.h`, one hook in `update_entity` (`local_game.h`); every grounded / uncrouched / no-wall / no-momentum path is unchanged (covered by control cases in `make test-wall-ride`). Works on axis-aligned and rotated/ramped walls. Verified headless only (unit test + server builds); NOT feel-tested in a live match, no wall-jump/HUD/camera cue yet, tuning constants (`PHYS_WALL_RIDE_*`) are first guesses.

- ci(release): SLIM CLIENT artifacts (card #621) -- `ShankPit_SlimClient_Windows_<build>.zip` + `ShankPit_SlimClient_Linux_<build>.zip`: client + SDL libs + assets/ + CONSTRUCT only (no World/server, Bots, or SHANKPIT OS `bundled/` apps), own upload + release assets. Bundle logic simulated locally; the real CI run is not verified yet.

# Changelog

## 2026-10-16
- feat(apps): PELLEGRINO wired into the lobby's APPS launcher page as `APP_PELLEGRINO` (`apps/lobby/src/main.c`), same "spawn the real, unmodified native binary as a child process" convention PITVIPER/IDUNA.GAME/REDGARDEN already use -- never a reimplementation of its UI inside SHANKPIT, so it automatically inherits PELLEGRINO's own real keybinds (`:` address bar, Tab/Shift+Tab link select, Enter/Space follow, F11/`p` markdown toggle, F12/`x` CSS toggle, Esc/q quit, Shift+J/L/I/K window move, PageUp/PageDown/Home/End + mouse wheel scroll) with no remapping needed. Founder real-time, 2026-10-16: "add another phone app for PELLEGRINO (clone it down), have it match the ui of all the other phone apps and give it the keybinds from pellegrino... it must function as a real browser." PELLEGRINO (`emilyspringerton/PELLEGRINO`) cloned down as a new sibling repo (was not previously checked out on this box, a named gap since its own `PELLEGRINO-GOLDEN-SYNC`/`V16-GOLDEN-SYNC` backlog items) -- distinct from this same file's own `OS menu is HTML + CSS painted by PELLEGRINO` entry below (`packages/pellegrino_embed/`, PELLEGRINO rendering the lobby's own menu chrome): this entry is PELLEGRINO as a *launchable app in its own window*, the two integrations are complementary, not overlapping. Real, honest verification done this session (not assumed): no Bazel/SDL2-dev/SDL2_ttf-dev/Xvfb/ImageMagick existed in this sandbox either, all installed (`libsdl2-dev`, `libsdl2-ttf-dev`, `libglu1-mesa-dev`, `xvfb`, `imagemagick`); PELLEGRINO's pinned PARENA commit (`c146f7e`, per `PELLEGRINO/MODULE.bazel`) built standalone (this sandbox's own PARENA checkout is newer and fails compiling `net/stream.prn` under the current, stricter `let`-placement rule, so the compiler was built from a pinned sibling checkout instead of the live one); `pellegrino.prn` + its stdlib deps (string/array/io/sdl2/bytes/inflate/png/image-store/net-stream + the full net/quic_wire+h3_wire+quic_crypto+quic_tls+quic_client chain, needed for symbol declarations even though QUIC/TLS themselves are stubbed out of this build) compiled clean to C via the pinned compiler, then linked directly with `gcc` (bypassing Bazel entirely, no mbedTLS in this build -- plain HTTP only, matching the feature's own staged rollout) against the pinned PARENA runtime + Monocypher + SDL2/SDL2_ttf. Real, live-verified under Xvfb: launched against `http://example.com/`, fetched the real page over the network, and rendered it on the glass -- screenshot shows the actual page text, URL in the status bar, and the `:=go to URL esc=quit css:on md:on` keybind hint row, not a blank or crashed window. This confirms "must function as a real browser" literally, not just that the launcher entry exists. The verified binary is placed at `PELLEGRINO/pellegrino` (gitignored, a local sandbox build artifact, not a reproducible release build -- the real CI/Bazel/HTTPS/QUIC build is still the one that ships) so the lobby's `dev_relative_path = "../../PELLEGRINO/pellegrino"` resolves on this box. `apps/lobby/src/main.c` syntax-checked clean (`gcc -fsyntax-only`, zero new errors/warnings); a full `make lobby` link was not attempted -- a pre-existing, unrelated gap (no `EDITOR.GAME` sibling checkout on this box, needed by `editor_widget_bridge.c`'s own generated-stdlib dependency, nothing to do with this change). Disk space was a real, hit blocker this session (`/home` at 100%, 0 bytes free) -- cleared reproducible caches only (`.npm/_cacache`, `.cache/go-build`, `.cache/pip`, ~400M reclaimed), nothing from any repo's own tracked source. CI (`release.yml`) does not yet cross-compile/bundle PELLEGRINO as a sixth sibling app -- named follow-up.

New `packages/pellegrino_embed/` (generated engine + vendored PARENA runtime + `pg_rename.h` so its symbols never collide with `packages/world/parena_runtime.c`), `scripts/gen_pellegrino_embed.sh`, `make test-os-menu` / `test-os-menu-gl`. `SHANKPIT_OS_MENU=classic` keeps the old glRectf grid (also the automatic fallback). - feat(release): `ShankPit_Client_Lite_<build>.zip` -- same directory structure as the full client zip but without `bundled/` (the SHANKPIT OS apps), for fast iteration; the regular zip still includes everything. Written into `release.yml`, not yet run in CI (kanban #622).
- fix(brick): damage now works on walls of nested/composed levels -- `BF_MAX_PARENTS` was 128 (and `BfRec.parent` a u8), but composed-object walls are flattened after the root walls, so anything past box 127 was never destructible (no damage, no carved render, in ZOMBIES etc). Now 2048 (= `LEVEL_BOXES_MAX`), record parent widened to u16 (wire already u16); regression check in `brick_fracture_test.c`. `brick_world_test.c` had pre-existing `-Werror` failures unrelated to this. Not verified in a live nested level (kanban #628).
- fix(stream): ffmpeg is no longer bundled in the Windows client zip and the exe-dir lookup is gone -- the game uses `ffmpeg` on PATH; new `docs/FFMPEG_INSTALL.md` (robust Windows winget/manual-PATH + troubleshooting, Linux per-distro); in-game message points to it; README updated (kanban #618).
- fix(levels): re-baked `baked_levels_gen.h` (32 -> 64 levels). The native client serves the baked registry by default and the 2026-10-04 bake predated cube rotation/ramp, so NOCK-rotated cubes never appeared rotated in game (render + physics for rot_*/ramp were already in place).
- feat(edit-sync): joining a live NOCK edit session (`--edit-session <id>`) loads the session's working level -- the editor's unsaved draft -- instead of the default map, and re-syncs on every NOCK `level` event (hero position kept; local op log dropped, NOCK is authoritative). `edit_sync_fetch_level` in `packages/world/edit_sync.h`. Not compiled/run here (no SDL2 on this box); header + test_edit_sync build clean.

- feat(render): weather finally reaches scene lighting, not just the sky dome -- new `sky_weather_light_state` (`packages/render/sky_weather.{h,c}`), closing a gap named since the 2026-09-22 engine-merge pass (`docs2/specs/BIGO_ENGINE_MERGE_NORTHSTAR.md` §3.1) and never picked up since. Adjusts the already-evaluated `RetroLightingState` in place using `SkyWeather`'s own already-smoothed `cover`/`storm` (dims ambient/sun/moon, floored so storm-at-noon is dim daylight not night) and `fog_wash`/`visibility`/`fog[3]` (blends world fog toward the sky's real weather-matched colour, tightens draw distance toward real visibility) -- the same numbers already driving the dome/cloud/precip visuals, reused rather than re-derived. ## 2026-10-01

- **MODE_TYLER voice, step 1: the cold open's actors now actually walk** (founder real-time: "ensure TYLER/MODE_TYLER affordances, VALHANNA voices via TTS"; plan `docs2/specs/TYLER_VOICE_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md`). Three real, pre-existing P0 blockers found by running the coordinator against the real engine (`make test-tyler-vo`, new `tests/tyler/test_tyler_vo.c`, wired into `tests.yml`): (1) `story_ai_tick` was a silent no-op in `MODE_TYLER` (gated to `MODE_STORY`), so on the dedicated server Tyler and Hana never received any movement input -- the NORTHSTAR's "live-verified" had only checked log lines; (2) `local_game.h` had no `MODE_TYLER` handling (init branch, `story_ai_tick` call, NPC movement branch, dead-state skip), so a local match would have run `bot_think` combat AI on the actors; (3) NOT in the plan, found while testing (1): `story_ai.c`'s `ai_angle_to` used a yaw convention exactly 180 degrees opposite the simulation's movement basis (`shared_movement.h`/`physics.h`: forward = (-sin yaw, -cos yaw)), so every `story_ai` NPC walked, turned and aimed directly AWAY from its target (a marker at z=-30 sent the actor to z=+50) -- fixed in the one place the convention lives (`atan2f(-dx, -dz)`); this also corrects `MODE_STORY` NPCs, which had never been verified to approach anything. Also: an actor a beat does not name now keeps its locked scripted state (long hold) instead of reverting to ally AI (which backed away from the wisp and fired at it). Tyler/Hana now run the full 8-beat choreography to the exit in a local match (headless test: 23 assertions). The two level JSONs moved from `examples/tyler-valhanna/` to `assets/tyler_levels/` (shipped in the client zip; offline fallback).
- **MODE_TYLER voice, step 2: committed VO assets + one generated source for voice and subtitles.** Eight Piper-rendered clips (Tyler: `fr_FR-tom-medium`, Hana: `fr_FR-siwis-medium`; Piper is a LABELED STOPGAP generator -- PARENA-native synthesis is the real work) resampled to 22050 Hz mono PCM16 (the engine's device rate; Tyler's source was 44100 Hz) are committed under `assets/tyler_vo/` (1.07 MiB, `MANIFEST.sha256` = sha256/bytes/duration per clip; Piper is not deterministic -- the same line rendered twice differs ~10% -- so they are never rendered in CI). `TYLER/tts/render_vh01.py` now reads one TSV (`vh01_beats.tsv`: French, English gloss, hold) and generates `packages/simulation/tyler_voice_lines.h` (lines table with offsets, per-beat `g_tyler_voice_clip_ms`, and the HUD subtitle for every beat), replacing `tyler_voice_clips.h`; the HUD subtitle table in `tyler_coldopen.c` now takes its strings from that header, which fixes a real mismatch: beat 0's voice says an extra sentence ("Compte les sorties avant de compter quoi que ce soit d'autre") that the subtitle box never showed. New `TylerVoiceLine g_tyler_voice_lines[]` in `tyler_coldopen.{h,c}`. `make test-tyler-vo` (109 assertions) now also checks every clip against the manifest (own SHA-256), duration vs table within 1 ms, per-beat `max(offset+dur) == clip_ms`, and that on the real coordinator's timeline every line ends >= 470 ms before the next beat.
- **MODE_TYLER voice, step 3: PARENA owns the playback decisions.** `PARENA/stdlib/tyler/voice_mod.prn` gains `on-tyler-voice-line-state` (pending/playing/over), `on-tyler-voice-seek-ms`, `on-tyler-duck-target-permille`, `on-tyler-duck-step-permille` (21 new assertions in PARENA `make test-tyler-voice`, PARENA `8eebf20`); `packages/simulation/tyler_voice_mod.c` is regenerated from it by the new `scripts/gen_tyler_voice_mod.sh`. New SDL-free `TylerBeatView` + `tyler_coldopen_get_view` (reports the previous beat across the advance-tick/trigger-tick gap so elapsed is never measured against a stale start) and `TylerVoiceDriver`/`tyler_voice_driver_step`, an idempotent edge-driver that turns a stream of views -- local every frame, over the wire later -- into start/seek/stop commands: a repeated view never retriggers a line, a line already over when first seen (late joiner) is never replayed, a line first seen mid-clip starts seeked, a beat jump cuts the old audio. Tested on the real coordinator (every line starts on its beat's dispatch tick + offset) and on late-join/duplicate/lost-packet/restart cases (`make test-tyler-vo`, 144 assertions).
- **Destructible brick** (founder real-time: "get papercraft tech shipped to shankpit i want brick to be destructable"). Brick level boxes now have per-cell HP (~3-unit cells, sparse -- a cell costs nothing until it is hurt); PAPERCRAFT's Paper Engine decisions (`paper_fragment_mod` material resistance + HP tiers, `interact_falloff_mod` distance falloff) plus new `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/brick_rules.prn` (weapon-vs-brick: blades 0, sniper x1.25, rockets x5; debris counts; brick = concrete-grade 80 HP) are compiled into `packages/simulation/brick_mod.c` (`scripts/gen_brick_mod.sh`). A destroyed cell becomes a real hole: `packages/world/brick_fracture.h` re-expresses each carved box's surviving volume as ordinary boxes by recursive bisection along the cell grid and `physics.h` writes them into the same custom-level collision/render slots (new `CUSTOM_LEVEL_FRACTURE_SLOTS`, authored boxes hidden, texture phase kept via per-piece uv offsets), so movement/hitscan/rockets/lighting needed no other change. Hooks: `update_weapons` (hitscan, pellets owned by the hook), `explode_splash` (blasts centred into the struck face), point projectiles, `local_init_match` (walls whole again each match). Server is the only authority; new `PACKET_BRICK_STATE` (13) carries idempotent `(box, cell key, hp)` entries, each change sent 3x plus a rotating refresh of all damaged cells (late joiners/lost datagrams converge, no acks); clients validate everything and only mirror. Client: papercraft-style debris flakes (`packages/world/brick_debris.h`), bullet-hole decals vanish with their cell, pause-menu BRICK DEBRIS toggle, `draw_map` skips hidden boxes and keeps brick-course alignment across piece seams. Tests: `make test-brick` (engine, hand-derived cases + 400-step fuzz with rollback, forced piece-budget overflow; real-engine integration through `update_weapons`/`trace_map`/slots/net apply/hostile packets -- both ASan+UBSan), `make brick-e2e` (real `shank_server` + real packet clients: shooter destroys a crate cell, late joiner learns it from the refresh) -- all wired into `tests.yml`. Live-verified under Xvfb on the new registry level **TUTORIAL_BRICK** (crate shatters, debris flies, hole through the wall). Honest limits: NOCK/custom-level maps only; melee does not hurt brick; floors/slabs, doors and non-brick materials are excluded; one shared 900-piece budget per level (over-budget damage is refused whole, never half-applied); BUILD.bazel entries added but Bazel not run here.
- CI source lists derive from the Makefile (root-cause fix for the 3-day red release pipeline; this drift had recurred NINE times): new `make -s print-lobby-src` / `print-server-src` targets; the Windows lobby and Linux server gcc lines in `tests.yml` and `release.yml` now expand them instead of carrying hand-copied `.c` lists. Verified the two sets were identical before the swap and that the expanded lists pass `-fsyntax-only` under mingw/gcc. A new `packages/*.c` added to LOBBY_SRC/SERVER_SRC no longer needs a CI edit.
- Release pipeline fixed after 3 days red (founder real-time: "i think the shankpit build is still down i dont have a new release yet from 3 days"): `release.yml`'s hand-copied Windows `dw_gui.exe` gcc line was missing `core/runtime/parena_runtime.c` (DEADWEIGHT's PARENA-generated `core/account_rules.c` calls `arena_alloc`) -> `undefined reference to arena_alloc`; together with the two earlier source-list fixes (`tyler_voice_mod.c`, `core/account_rules.c`) the full "Build SHANKPIT OS Apps (Windows)" step was reproduced locally from clean `git archive` trees of all five sibling repos with real SDL2+SDL2_ttf mingw kits (all five .exe link) before pushing. `v0.136.0` published (client zip verified: ShankPit.exe + bundled dw_gui/pitviper/idunagame/red_garden_arena/editor-game .exe + SDL2/SDL2_ttf DLLs) -- the first release containing the bullet-hole decals.
- Pause menu is now a real options menu (founder standing rule: "always ensure all features have menu items"): RESUME / BULLET HOLES: ON|OFF / FULLSCREEN: ON|OFF / QUIT TO LOBBY. The bullet-hole toggle is persisted as line 2 of `shankpit_display.cfg` (older one-line configs stay ON); decals keep tracking while hidden. Live-verified under Xvfb (SOLO -> ESC -> toggle -> cfg `0\n0`).

- Per-gun bullet-hole decals (founder real-time: "shooting a wall causes a bullet hole, different per gun ... like half life ... using the nock tools parena texture generator and database"). Each gun's hole is a PARENA program (`PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/textures/bullet_hole_{magnum,ar,shotgun,sniper}.prn`) rendered by NOCK and stored as `bullet-hole-<gun>` rows in IDUNA's `nock_textures`; the client fetches the live NOCK image (IDUNA `GET /api/v1/nock-textures/by-name/<name>/image`) and falls back to the checked-in export (`packages/world/bullet_hole_gen.h`, regenerate with `scripts/gen_bullet_holes.sh`). New `packages/world/bullet_hole.h` (shot detection from per-player ammo drops -- works for local prediction and networked snapshots with no new wire field; shotgun = 8 spread rays; 256-hole ring buffer, per-hole random rotation/size), `apps/lobby` traces each shot into the map (`trace_map`) and draws multiply-blended quads. `make test-bullet-hole` (headless). Live-verified under Xvfb: four visibly different holes per gun. Honest limits: map geometry only (a shot that hits a player also marks the wall behind), no per-material variation, live fetch needs the IDUNA route deployed (embedded export used until then). Vendored `parena_runtime.h` gained `bytes_slice_impl`; `png_decode_gen.c` regenerated for paletted PNGs.

  named follow-up ("Phase 1's clock currently ticks off client-local wall-clock time in
  apps/lobby; not yet ticked server-side nor broadcast in a snapshot packet, so two clients would
  see two different times of day"). `local_state.story_clock` (new `DayNightClock` field on
  `ServerState`, `packages/common/protocol.h`) is now the one real clock for MODE_STORY/
  MODE_STORY_CAVE: `local_init_match` seeds it fresh per match, `local_game.h`'s `local_update`
  advances it for a local (non-networked) match, and a new `apps/server/src/main.c` per-tick block
  advances + broadcasts it (`PACKET_WORLD_CLOCK`/`NetWorldClock`, a real, separate packet that
  never touches `server_broadcast`'s own snapshot buffer at all) for a genuine networked story
  session -- not yet live-deployed anywhere (`shankpit-server.service` runs `--deathmatch` only).
  Every other mode (deathmatch etc., which draws the same weather-aware sky purely as a visual)
  keeps ticking its own client-local fallback exactly as before -- zero behavior change for the
  actually-live mode, confirmed via a real scratch integration harness (not committed, ASan/UBSan
  clean): `server_broadcast_world_clock()` is a genuine no-op for MODE_DEATHMATCH, sends the real,
  correct packet for MODE_STORY.
  **Real bug found and fixed along the way**: the clock's own original tick math
  (`(int)(dt_sec * DAY_NIGHT_MINUTES_PER_REAL_SEC)`) truncated to 0 on every normal-framerate call
  (a single ~16ms frame is 0.016 real seconds, always below the 1.0 needed for even one whole
  minute at the 1:1 rate this repo uses) -- this clock had likely never actually advanced in real
  gameplay at all via this mechanism, in any mode, since it first landed. Fixed in all three tick
  sites (the pre-existing lobby fallback, the new `local_update` path, the new server path) by
  carrying the fractional remainder forward instead of discarding it every call; the live harness
  confirms ~5 real seconds of realistic 16ms ticks now correctly advances the clock ~5 sim minutes.
  `make server`/`make lobby` and `bazel build //...` (minus the pre-existing, unrelated
  `serverctl`/ncurses gap) both clean. No live service restarted or redeployed -- see
  `EMILY/BACKLOG.md` for the deploy step, a real, human-supervised decision, same discipline every
  other live-service change in this monorepo follows.

## 2026-09-24
- fix(sim): **root-caused and fixed the SHANKPIT queue crash loop** named as an open item earlier
  today. Reproduced live under gdb (`gdb --batch -ex run -ex "bt full" --args ./bin/shank_server
  --deathmatch`): SIGSEGV in `__vfprintf_internal`, `s=0x0`, called from `witness_ai_tick ->
  witness_sim_tick`. Root cause: `witness_sim.c`'s ~20 `fprintf(s->out, ...)` debug/event-log
  calls all assumed `WitnessSim.out` was always a valid `FILE*`, but the only place that ever
  sets it (`witness_ai_reset`, via `witness_ai.c`'s `g_sim_log`) is never called anywhere in
  either `apps/server/src/main.c` or `apps/lobby/src/main.c` -- only `witness_sim_test.c` calls
  it (always with `stdout`). The dedicated server calls `witness_ai_tick` unconditionally every
  tick (wired in during the BIG_O engine merge, 2026-09-20) with a 1s internal timer gate on
  `witness_sim_tick`, whose own doc comment claims "a real no-op whenever nothing is spawned" --
  true for the witness/zombie population logic, false for this one unconditional log line, which
  crashed the very first time the 1s gate opened on ANY mode/level, story or not. Fix: added a
  `sim_log()` helper + `SIM_LOG` macro that no-ops on a NULL sink instead of dereferencing it,
  replaced all 23 `fprintf(s->out, ...)` call sites (mechanical, `witness_sim.c` only --
  `witness_sim_print_state`'s own separate `FILE *out` param is untouched, it was never the
  problem). This makes the existing "safe no-op" doc comment actually true rather than adding new
  behavior -- `g_sim`'s other fields are already harmlessly zero (`nplayers=0`) on this path, so
  witness AI simply stays inert outside `MODE_STORY`+VOXWORLD as designed, it just no longer
  crashes getting there. Verified: `make server` clean, `./bin/shank_server --deathmatch` and
  `--port 6971 --level var/zombie/nextown_zombies.json` both ran 8s directly (previously ~1s to
  SEGV every time), then the real `shankpit-server.service` + `shankpit-zombie.service` +
  `shankpit-bot-pool.service` (8 bots) all restarted and held stable 30+s with zero crashes and
  all 8 bots actually connecting (previously 100% "failed to connect" once the server started
  dying). Closes `EMILY/BACKLOG.md` SECTION 541's open item.
- feat(lobby): new **ZOMBIES** entry on the APPS page (`apps/lobby/src/main.c`, `AppsAction`/
  `APPS_LABELS`) -- founder real-time: "add a new button to shankpit menu (in apps) to join the
  shankpit zombie zerver game." Not a separate binary like the other five app entries: `lobby_launch_app`
  now special-cases `APP_ZOMBIES` to self-relaunch `shank_lobby` (`/proc/self/exe` on Linux,
  `GetModuleFileNameA`+`CreateProcessA` on the Windows cross-build) with `--host 127.0.0.1 --port
  6971`, landing in a fresh lobby already pointed at the zombie sandbox server
  (`shankpit-zombie.service`) instead of the default queue port 6969 -- Join still needs a click
  from there, same "still needs its own further step" precedent REDGARDEN's own app entry already
  set. `make lobby` builds clean, no new warnings. README's Apps page section updated (was stale
  at "currently just DEADWEIGHT" even before this).
- ops(queue): founder real-time "check on shankpit matchmaking, reduce the queue bot pool to 8" --
  while checking, found the real problem wasn't the bot pool: `shankpit-server.service` (the live
  queue server, UDP :6969) is in an active SIGSEGV crash-restart loop, dying ~1s after every
  restart (deterministic: 3x `[BUGGY] spawned`, `[STADIUM] terrain initialized`, then SEGV --
  restart counter climbing continuously, e.g. 63->70 across two minutes of observation).

## 2026-09-21
- fix(net): PFSP/self-play RL training could hang forever on `env.reset()` -- founder real-time: "we need to fix SHANKPIT PFSP pipeline it dont work." Live-reproduced the exact "TimeoutError: no live respawn snapshot within timeout" running the real pipeline (`scripts/rl_train_packet.py` + real `shank_server` + real `emily-bot`) -- confirmed via a fresh compiled `sizeof(NetPlayer)` probe that the 84->88-byte wire-mirror drift already fixed 2026-09-17 (`aa57dda`) is still correctly in place and NOT the cause. Real root cause, found via server logs: under real CPU contention (a concurrent PPO training process starving the bot/server's own scheduling), a client can get idle-timed-out server-side (`apps/server/src/main.c`'s 5s `SLOT_TIMEOUT`) and then have one more already-in-flight `PACKET_USERCMD` land before its own client-side reconnect logic notices -- `ensure_slot_for_sender()` let that bare USERCMD (not just `PACKET_CONNECT`) silently mint a brand-new slot for the unrecognized sender, which never goes through `send_welcome()` and so sits permanently `welcomed=0` (but `cmd_seen=1`, so it looks "connected" in server status) -- a real, permanent phantom-slot livelock, distinct from the earlier wire-format bug. Fixed: `ensure_slot_for_sender` now takes an `allow_alloc` flag, true only for `PACKET_CONNECT`; a stray USERCMD/DISCONNECT from an unrecognized sender is dropped instead of minting a phantom slot, forcing a real reconnect handshake. Verified deterministically (not timing-dependent): a raw USERCMD sent from a never-connected UDP socket now produces zero slot activity (`Clients: 0`, no `slot=` line at all) where before the fix it would have created one. `go build ./...` clean, `go test ./apps2/emily-bot/...` (13 tests) and `python3 -m unittest discover scripts` (39 tests) all green. (sess-20260920-1908-24cb3558)

## 2026-09-18
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike now writes a real get-up ORACLE .gband clip, correcting a mis-scope (S497) -- founder real-time: "fuck it play the death animation backwards - let the robot learn how to just pop back up itll still be horiffic but in a different way," then, correcting the first cut of this before any code shipped: "my bro the rag doll doesnt need to stand up if its not rigid body it wont fall over - reversing the death animation was supposed to be the oracle that the RL learns against to be able to stand back up after falling over." tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c now records every tick's joint positions during its forward fall sim and, after the sim completes, walks the buffer backward and writes a real `.gband` binary + manifest (tools/ragdoll_spike/output/getup_oracle.gband(.json), gitignored -- generated, not source) -- an imitation-reward reference clip for HQ-SPEC-SIM-100 §4's Reward Compiler, not a directly-playable get-up animation (this spike has no rigid-body/orientation state, so nothing in it actually "fell over" in the sense that would make literal reverse playback a usable animation on its own). Verified end to end against GOLDENBAND's own real reader, not just the writer's own claim: loaded via gb_init, gb_verify PASSes (content hash matches), duration_ticks=192, num_channels=195 (65 joints x tx/ty/tz), and the arc runs from the fall's settled/lying pose (joint0 y=0.000) at tick 0 up to the original standing rest pose (joint0 y=7.987) at the final tick -- confirms the reversed clip actually shapes a lying-to-standing arc. docs2/RAGDOLL_ORIENTATION_NORTHSTAR.md updated with the corrected framing as a real third recovery path (alongside the existing authored-blend and trained-policy paths), and directly answers the founder's separate "most importantly we need a roll over animation" point: not needed for this specific mechanism, since the reversed clip retraces whatever orientation the character actually fell into, not a fixed assumed starting pose -- a roll-over step is still a real, separate need for any OTHER recovery path that assumes a fixed pose. Compiles clean under -Wall.
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike iteration 3 (ground friction) + real orientation-physics NORTHSTAR (S495/S496) -- founder real-time chain: "we may as well see how far we can push the engine while we are building it it impacts the kinds of stories we can tell" -> "and then what does it look like to take it all the way to robotics? do we have to build cad in?" -> "if the character falls over theres no way its going to look human like when they get up its going to be horiffic and synthetic." Tested one more real, cheap hypothesis before committing to full orientation physics: added GROUND_FRICTION (kills horizontal implied-velocity on ground contact, previously only vertical was killed). Result: real but partial -- Y-flatness completely unchanged, but lateral spread tightened substantially (fingertip spread ~0.6-0.7 -> ~0.3-0.5 units), turning a splayed starfish into a more compact pile. Real, corrected framing reached in the same conversation: lying flat after falling isn't actually the bug (real ragdolls do that) -- the real problem is the symmetric, poker-straight shape, since nothing resists a joint straightening toward full extension. New docs2/RAGDOLL_ORIENTATION_NORTHSTAR.md: real v0 design (swing-axis constraints derived from rest-pose geometry, no new .gskel metadata needed) for the actual next increment, phased (major joints first), with twist limits/full per-bone orientation named honestly as the bigger v1 lift. Directly answers two real, adjacent founder questions in the same doc: confirms "horrific and synthetic" recovery is a well-documented real RL failure mode, HQ-SPEC-SIM-100's own Reward Compiler (imitation reward against a real reference clip) is the actual fix, names two real recovery paths (authored blend now vs. trained policy later); and grounds the robotics/CAD question directly in SIM-100 §3's own real actuator-metadata (torque/velocity/backlash) requirement, sourced from real hardware specs, not full CAD in the simulator.

## 2026-09-17
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike iteration 2 -- real per-bone mass + bend constraints, a sharply-defined Phase 2 finding (S494) -- founder real-time: "just work on ragdoll physics" / "we are going to need to define masses for objects im sure for rigid body physics." Added real per-bone mass to tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c (proportional to each joint's own parent-bone length -- a thigh genuinely outweighs a finger bone now, replacing the original uniform inv_mass=1) and a real bend constraint (BendConstraint, a one-sided minimum-distance constraint between a joint and its own grandparent -- the standard PBD bend-resistance technique). Live-verified the bend constraint works exactly as designed: 0/63 constraints violated at the final settled state, real hyperextension/fold-back genuinely prevented. But the whole body still collapses completely flat (every joint at y=0.000) by t=0.75s, unchanged from iteration 1 -- a real, conclusive, NOT-a-tuning-problem finding: a fully-extended limb lying flat on the ground satisfies every distance AND bend constraint simultaneously, since neither has any concept of orientation relative to gravity, only relative distances between points. No number or combination of point-distance constraints can ever fix this. Real, now sharply-defined Phase 2: a believable ragdoll needs actual per-bone orientation state and real angular/swing-twist joint limits (true rigid-body dynamics with quaternion orientations), a categorically different, larger technique than point-mass PBD -- not an incremental extension of what's built.
- feat(render): real enclosed/indoor lighting + real screen-space bloom (S493) -- founder real-time, from a real in-game screenshot: "theres not much difference between having lights on and not having lights - its still basically illuminated in this totally enclosed level ... there should be more difference between light and dark and we should see like the lighting kind of move a bit with the glow effect of the hps light." Two real, found root causes, both fixed:
  1. **Enclosed/indoor lighting**: `retro_lighting_eval_surface_rgb` (packages/render/retro_lighting.c) adds a real, physically-motivated sun/moon "scattered sky" fill term to every wall face unconditionally -- correct for outdoor scenes, but applied even to windowless interior rooms with zero real sky exposure, swamping the real per-fixture (HPS/IPS) point-light contrast a level author actually placed. `RETRO_LIGHTING_INTERIOR_FLAT` already existed as a defined preset (zero sun/moon contribution) but was never actually selected anywhere in the codebase -- a real, found-live "defined but orphaned" gap. New `CustomLevelData.enclosed` (level_boxes.h, real JSON key, 0/absent default = unchanged outdoor behavior) drives a real switch in `level_boxes_apply_to_physics` (apps/lobby's own shared level-load path, covers local single-player, the --level CLI flag, AND the network multiplayer client's own QUEUE-level fetch, all three call sites in one place) -- `g_world_lighting_preset` now actually changes per level for the first time ever (previously a single global, hardcoded to RETRO_LIGHTING_DAY_STATIC, exactly ONE assignment in the entire codebase). Real safety net added: leaving an enclosed level for a built-in outdoor scene (VOXWORLD etc.) resets the preset back, so the world doesn't stay stuck dim. IDUNA's own new SetEnclosed method + NOCK checkbox let a designer toggle this per level -- see that repo's CHANGELOG. 6/6 new tests green (apps/tests/test_level_enclosed_lighting.c).
  2. **Real screen-space bloom** (new packages/render/bloom.h/.c) -- the "glow" that already existed (draw_light_glow_billboard) was a camera-facing sprite at each fixture's own position, not true bloom: didn't react to what's actually bright on screen, didn't bleed past its own fixed size. Real technique now: render the frame to an off-screen FBO, extract genuinely bright pixels via a real luminance threshold (a real GLSL shader, naturally picks up the already-bright HPS/IPS emissive surfaces AND their existing per-frame flicker with zero extra wiring), blur via a real 2-pass separable Gaussian (quarter-res, a real, deliberate glow-radius/perf tradeoff) using its own second GLSL shader, composite back onto the real frame with additive blending. New FBO/renderbuffer GL extension loading (own p_gl* set, same hand-loaded SDL_GL_GetProcAddress convention gl_shader.c already established, for the same real Windows-cross-compile portability reason). Handles window resize (SDL_WINDOW_RESIZABLE) by reallocating targets when size changes. Real, found-and-fixed bug in an early draft: gl_shader.h has no glUniform2fv-equivalent helper, and the first cut called mismatched-arity setters (gl_uniform1f/gl_uniform3fv) against the blur shader's own vec2 uniform -- a real, silent GL_INVALID_OPERATION no-op that would have left the blur direction at a permanent (0,0), i.e. sampling the same texel 5 times and never actually blurring anything. Fixed by using two real scalar uniforms instead. Deliberately whole-frame (HUD/weapon-viewmodel/overlay elements bloom too, a real accepted tradeoff given this game's own existing neon/retro-CRT aesthetic, not an oversight) -- wraps draw_scene's entire existing call site with zero changes to draw_scene's own body. Real, honest, degrades safely: bloom_init's own failure (old driver/software renderer) makes every other bloom_* call a genuine no-op, confirmed live under Xvfb's own llvmpipe software GL (which lacks the needed shader entry points in this sandbox, a real, pre-existing, already-documented limitation elsewhere in this same codebase) -- the binary ran the full test duration with zero crash, exactly the intended honest degradation. Actual bloom visual quality needs verification on real GPU/driver hardware, a real, named limitation of this sandbox (no display), same as this session's own earlier day/night lighting work.
  make server/make lobby both clean.
- fix(ai): AI_ROLE_WANDERING_BOT no longer silently rejected at level load (S492) -- founder real-time: "how do i stat to have different characters like walking around the city and stuff - even just standing there and having their head turn and look at you and they can say something or whatever." Real, found-live gap: this role already exists natively and already does almost exactly this (patrols, turns to face + waves + dances when the player gets close, never combat) -- but apps/server/src/main.c's and apps/lobby/src/main.c's own character-spawn range checks both stopped one enum value short at AI_ROLE_BLIND_STALKER, so a level author who managed to author role=10 anyway would have had that character silently skipped ("CUSTOM_LEVEL_CHARACTER_SKIPPED reason=invalid_role"), not spawned as the real, working ambient bot it actually is. Both range checks widened to AI_ROLE_WANDERING_BOT. Same real gap fixed on IDUNA's own Go mirror (internal/shankpit.validateCharacters) and, separately, in NOCK's own frontend dropdown (see that repo's CHANGELOG) -- three independent copies of the same off-by-one, all found and fixed together. make server/make lobby both clean.
- feat(sim): level exits can target a specific destination spawner, for seamless GTA-style building interiors (S491) -- founder real-time: "so i figured out we can do buildings like GTA we have an exit point of the level that is pointed to the level that represents the interior of that building - exiting that level you can spawn back into the world but how can i specify which spawner the exit leads to for the seamless experience of exiting the building." New LevelExit.target_spawner_id (level_boxes.h, 0 default = unchanged normal team/FFA spawner selection): when set, an exit overrides the destination level's spawner pick with one exact, author-chosen spawner by its own real id -- e.g. a building's front door leading to the exact spot just outside it, instead of a random spawner across the map. Real, found-live gap fixed along the way: LevelSpawner never actually parsed its own author-assigned "id" from JSON at all (silently dropped, since nothing needed it before this) -- fixed (LevelSpawner.id, JSON parsing, threaded through phys_set_custom_level_spawners' now-5-arg signature and both its real call sites in apps/server and apps/lobby). New custom_level_pick_spawner_by_id: a real, minimal, additive sibling to the existing team/FFA custom_level_pick_spawner, honestly returning 0 (never crashing) for a stale/missing target id or the real target_spawner_id<=0 "no target" sentinel. story_check_level_exits (server) and lobby_check_story_level_exits (lobby) both capture which exit actually fired and, after phys_respawn's own normal full reset, surgically override just the resulting position when a target spawner is named and still exists. 10/10 new tests green (apps/tests/test_level_exit_spawner_target.c), make server/make lobby both clean.
- feat(sim): REFLUX cross-mod pub/sub, ported natively into SHANKPIT, plus real buttons and button-subscribing doors (S485) -- founder real-time: "we need buttons - how am i gonna put a button on a wall next to a door to open a door?" -> "we need a reusable button that can be put in different places ... i want to follow the pub sub model ... we need to use the same pub sub mod as ECOWAR" -> "bring in the ecowar pub sub mod" -> "but keep all of the code in shankpit". New packages/reflux/reflux_runtime.h/.c: a real, native port of ECOWAR's own proven REFLUX primitive (EMILY/BACKLOG.md SECTION 380/381) -- same API/ABI (reflux_host_dispatch/log_size/action_*_at), own log, zero cross-repo dependency. New packages/simulation/story_buttons.h: a real, reusable, edge-triggered interact button (reuses the ALREADY-WIRED BTN_USE input path -- an F-key binding that already flowed end to end to p->in_use/p->use_was_down for CTFB flag pickup, just never had a second consumer before) -- pressing one dispatches REFLUX_ACTION_BUTTON_PRESSED(button_id, player_id, 0) and knows nothing else. New LevelDoor.subscribe_button_id (level_boxes.h, -1 default = every existing door unchanged): a door with this set ignores distance-to-player entirely and instead polls the shared REFLUX log (its own persistent cursor, host-tracked since PARENA/VS0 has no closures for real push callbacks) for matching BUTTON_PRESSED actions, toggling open/closed on each one -- real agency ("a door that is open and closable gives you more agency"), not proximity hysteresis. Wired into BOTH the dedicated server (story_doors_tick) and local single-player (lobby_doors_tick, dlfcn-free so it works there too, unlike scripted PARENA doors) -- and the button-press dispatcher itself runs unconditionally, every game mode, per S481c's own resolved "levels are never story-mode-only" policy. Real, deliberate inversion of control, founder's own words: "the bridge listens for a button the button has no idea the bridge exists" -- proven end to end with a genuinely unrelated control door in the same test that a button press never touches. 13/13 new tests green (apps/tests/test_reflux_buttons.c), make server/make lobby both clean. Honest, explicitly named next phases, not built this pass: proximity sensors (REFLUX_ACTION_PROXIMITY_ENTER/EXIT, action types reserved but never dispatched anywhere yet) and gaze/look-at triggers (REFLUX_ACTION_LOOK_AT, same), plus a real PARENA-scripted REFLUX reactor (today's door-subscription toggle is a built-in C default, matching door_tick_builtin_proximity's own precedent -- a scriptable PARENA reactor contract is real future work, not this pass's own scope)
- feat(physics): ragdoll/rigid-body PBD spike (S484), throwaway validation, not shippable -- founder real-time: "can we add ragdoll and rigid body physics including stuff with mass? can we make the models based on the manequin we have for multiplayer?" New tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c: FKs the real mannequin_npc.gskel's rest pose (the exact same 65-joint skeleton already used for real multiplayer players via SKIN_MANNEQUIN, no new model needed), then runs a minimal Verlet/PBD point-mass + distance-constraint sim (gravity, ground plane, SHANKPIT's own real 64Hz tick rate) to test whether the approach is even stable on this engine's real skeleton data before any design doc gets written. Found and fixed two real bugs in the first cut: a Verlet ground-clamp velocity spike (clamped pos.y without clamping prev_pos.y, producing a teleport-velocity blowup) and a sign error in the distance-constraint correction for body B (pushed the constraint apart instead of together -- verified by hand-deriving the PBD update against C(pa,pb)=|pa-pb|-restLen). Result, real and conclusive: once both bugs were fixed, the sim is numerically stable (no NaN/divergence, converges by t=0.5s) at the real 65-joint/64-constraint scale and real tick rate -- PBD is a viable foundation. But it converges to every joint flat at y=0: distance-only constraints don't stop a knee/elbow folding backward, since bending never violates a bone-length constraint. Real, named next requirement: angular/joint-limit constraints, not per-bone collision shapes or mass tuning, are the actual next architecture question for a believable ragdoll. See tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c's own header for the full before/after (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(net): the REAL cause of "no live respawn snapshot within timeout" -- NetPlayer (protocol.h) grew from 84 to 88 bytes when anim_override (S470) was added, and both real wire-format mirrors (rl_env_packet.py's Python ctypes class, apps2/emily-bot/snapshot.go's netPlayerSize) were still at 84 -- the exact same class of bug this codebase has already hit and fixed twice before (S459-44, S459-69), each time from a hand-maintained mirror not updated when the real struct grew. With a stale, 4-byte-too-small stride, decode_snapshot correctly parses the FIRST entity in a multi-entity snapshot but reads every entity after it from the wrong offset -- so a real training client, which almost always connects as the SECOND participant (behind a heuristic bot or self-play opponent, exactly matching real production config), could never find itself among a snapshot's own entities. Every earlier reproduction attempt used a lone-client smoke test, which could never have caught this (only one entity = nothing for the stale stride to misalign). Found by finally reproducing the EXACT real config (real --save-freq, real registry push, --heuristic-opponents 1) instead of an isolated test, then dumping raw packet bytes instead of guessing further. Fixed all three consumers + their own stale self-checking assertions. go test ./apps2/emily-bot/... (13 tests) and python -m unittest discover (39 tests) both green. Live-verified conclusively: the identical repro (real server + real heuristic bot + a real second client) fails before this fix and succeeds immediately after, zero other changes (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(rl): per-generation training chunk (--save-freq) was ~5x too small and unconfigurable from Colab -- founder real-time, confirmed: "the traing was bad because the trainig wasnt going long enough." Defaulted to 4096 (only 2 real PPO rollout-collection+update cycles at SB3's own default n_steps=2048), nearly 5x smaller than BRAWLPIT/scripts/rl_train_packet.py's own analogous --save-freq=20_000 -- a real drift from the sibling implementation this file was explicitly built to mirror, not a deliberate choice, and a real, plausible explanation for the original S475 investigation's own "60 generations, flat/declining Elo" finding. colab_train.py also never exposed --save-freq as an overridable setting at all (every other real training knob already had a SHANKPIT_<NAME> env var passthrough, this one didn't). Fixed both: default raised to 20_000 (matching BRAWLPIT), new SHANKPIT_SAVE_FREQ env var passthrough in colab_train.py. python -m unittest discover (39 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(server): the per-tick [STATUS] log no longer floods under --fast-forward (S483 follow-up) -- founder pushed back hard on the earlier "couldn't reproduce it" answer, correctly ("are you sure? ... it is broke"), after a completely fresh Colab session with a freshly-pulled script still failed. Real root cause, found by reproducing with the ACTUAL game mode training uses (MODE_QUEUE, not MODE_DEATHMATCH -- earlier manual repro tests used the wrong mode) and measuring instead of guessing: the [STATUS] print throttled by tick count (tick % 600 == 0), a reasonable ~10s cadence at a normal server's ~60 ticks/sec, but --fast-forward decouples tick count from wall time (~500K ticks/sec measured live), so the same condition fires hundreds of times/sec even idle. Measured ~37KB/sec in a verbose build. This print used to go to DEVNULL (discarded instantly) until the same-day _spawn_server fix started capturing stdout to a real file for crash-diagnosis -- combined with this pre-existing fast-forward bug, writing that firehose continuously (worse on a network-backed --output-dir like a Colab Drive mount) for a multi-hour run is a real, serious I/O cost that plausibly stalls the server's own tick loop past a client's respawn window. Fixed at the source: real wall-clock throttling (net_should_log_every, same mechanism net_server_emit_summary already uses) instead of tick-count throttling. Live-verified both correctness (a real MODE_QUEUE client against the real TRAINING_GROUND level under --fast-forward still connects and stays alive exactly as before) and volume (~350x reduction, confirmed via real timed log measurements). make server/make lobby clean (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(rl): _spawn_server (rl_train_packet.py) no longer silences the real training server's stdout/stderr to DEVNULL -- founder real-time bug report: a Colab run's very first env.reset() raised "TimeoutError: no live respawn snapshot within timeout" with zero other diagnostic output. Same class of bug S459-61 already fixed for the two eval bots, left unfixed for the server itself. Now captures to a real log file (output_dir/server_<port>.log) and checks for an early process exit right after the startup grace period, raising an immediate, loud RuntimeError with the log tail instead of a silent, misleading 30s timeout downstream. Live-verified both the happy path (real server, real non-empty log) and the failure path (SERVER_BIN pointed at /bin/false raises the new RuntimeError). Honest, not fully resolved: could not reproduce the founder's own actual timeout locally against the real live TRAINING_GROUND level + matching flags -- a real client connected and got a valid alive snapshot in ~1s, no crash. This fix makes the NEXT occurrence diagnosable, doesn't explain this one (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)

- feat(physics): real per-material ground friction + global friction tuning bump (S478a/S478b) -- founder real-time: "how hard would it be to increase the friction some at the edges of blocks by default so its a little easier to stick to platforms? right now the physics are so slippery it can be unreasonably hard to parkour" -> "make the material friction stuff working per cube". Old per-box LevelBox.friction stays parsed-but-ignored legacy. Live-verified with a standalone C test: two synthetic materials (friction 0.02 vs 0.90) resolve correctly per-box and produce a dramatically different velocity-decay curve (0.81 vs 0.00 speed retained after 5 ticks). make server/make lobby clean (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
