## 2026-09-15
- S459-76: rl_train_packet.py now pushes updated Elo back onto already-registered checkpoints -- founder real-time: "the elos of the generation 0 bots arent going up and down... can we make sure the elos are set up to go up and down not just whatever the first elo into the registry is?" Real gap: record_match_result already correctly moves BOTH the new generation's and its prior generation's local Elo (a real, symmetric update), but the prior generation was pushed to the registry before that evaluation ran, and nothing ever pushed the update back -- a checkpoint's remote Elo was frozen at its push-time value forever. New rl_registry.py update_checkpoint_elo() calls IDUNA's new PATCH endpoint (see IDUNA CHANGELOG), wired in right after record_match_result using the already-tracked (previously unused) prev_remote_ids. Live-verified end to end: ran a real local training run with registry push, confirmed generation 0's rows moved from elo=1500 to real, distinct values (1484/1500/1516) after generation 1's own evaluation matches. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-75: new docs/ANTICHEAT_NORTHSTAR.md -- founder real-time: "northstar this for anticheat" (after a real research conversation on hardware-level USB HID input cheats, ATmega32U4/Arduino-based aimbots that anti-cheat software can't distinguish from a real mouse). Scopes a real, phased plan grounded directly in SHANKPIT's own existing architecture (server-authoritative combat, real UserCmd yaw/pitch telemetry already flowing through process_user_cmd at 60Hz, the real bot-pool infrastructure and S459-62's own active-opponent precedent) -- not a generic essay. Real design: Phase 0 passive telemetry (snap detection, consistency-under-fire scoring, no action taken yet), Phase 1 silent reroute of suspected players into a bot-only match, Phase 2 an escalating bot difficulty ladder culminating in sub-human-reaction-time "impossible" bots (a real, physically-grounded, near-unfakeable signal since no human can out-track a 0ms-reaction opponent), Phase 3 human-reviewed or bounded-cost outcomes, never an automatic permaban from this system alone. Named, not solved: screen-reading/CV-only cheats and memory-reading wallhacks are a different threat class this doc doesn't cover. NORTHSTAR only, no code written (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-74: fixed three real spray bugs -- founder real-time: "its really unreliable to do a spray you have to hit the button over and over... it jitters when you first use a spray... it sprays backwards and when you switch levels the sprays dont disappear they are floating in space where the walls used to be". (1) trace_map_boxes (backs both spray placement and the flashlight beam) was never a real raycast -- it only tested whether the segment's fixed FAR ENDPOINT happened to land inside a wall's volume, never a real segment-vs-box intersection, explaining exactly why facing a wall dead-on only worked at one specific standoff distance while running alongside it landed by chance. Replaced with a real closest-hit slab-method segment-vs-AABB test (same algorithm as scripts/rl_env_packet.py's own raycast, S459-73's own verified C-equivalent math). Live-verified with a direct C probe: 8 standoff distances from 1-35 units all now hit reliably at the exact correct wall surface with the correct normal; correct misses when pointed away or genuinely clear of the wall. (2) Sprays never got cleared on scene/level change, and every custom level shares the same SCENE_CUSTOM_LEVEL scene_id, so old sprays from a level you'd left kept rendering against geometry that no longer existed -- fixed by clearing the decal buffer in reset_client_render_state_for_net, the same real connect/reconnect boundary that already re-fetches level geometry. (3) spray_registry_fetch_list is a genuinely blocking network call (curl via popen) that used to fire inline on a player's first-ever T press, stalling the main loop mid-input and producing the reported jitter/backward-spray -- fixed by resolving the default spray proactively at scene entry (client_prefetch_default_spray, mirroring client_load_queue_level's own established prefetch pattern) instead of reactively on first use. apps/server and apps/lobby build clean, no new warnings. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests) and go test (all packages) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-73: real, measured additional speedup on raycast() -- founder real-time: "ok yea optimize more if you can" (after S459-72's numpy-scalar fix, cProfile showed raycast/_slab as the actual remaining bottleneck, 34% of build_observation). Two changes: (1) Wall now precomputes its own AABB bounds (lo_x/hi_x/etc) once at construction instead of every raycast() call recomputing `w.x - w.sx/2` etc from scratch on every one of the 5 raycasts build_observation makes per tick against the same unchanging wall list; (2) the per-axis slab test is now inlined directly into raycast()'s own loop instead of a separate _slab() function call per axis (real Python function-call + tuple pack/unpack overhead on a hot path -- 1.25M such calls per 50k build_observation calls in cProfile). _slab itself kept as a real, standalone, still-tested reference implementation, not deleted. Verified EXACT numerical equivalence, not just "close enough": 200k random-input trials comparing old vs new raycast() output, zero mismatches -- also locked in as a new permanent regression test. Real, measured result: build_observation dropped from 62.46us/call (post-S459-72) to 50.75us/call -- combined with S459-72, a real 34.5% speedup from the original 77.54us/call baseline. cProfile confirms raycast's own share dropped from 34% to 15% of build_observation's total time. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests, +1 new) and go test (all packages) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-72: real, measured 24% speedup on build_observation() -- founder real-time: "is python itself the bottleneck? do we need to write it in c?" Profiled a real env step first (2000 real steps against an isolated dev server): build_observation was 52-56% of total step time, NOT network I/O (34.8%) or GPU-relevant compute. Profiled inside build_observation itself: it called numpy's vectorized ufuncs (np.sin/cos/sqrt/arctan2/degrees/radians/tanh) on single scalar floats throughout -- a real, measured 5.7x-slower-than-necessary anti-pattern (direct microbenchmark: numpy scalar calls 2.64us vs math module 0.46us). Swapped all 14 real call sites to the stdlib math module. Live-verified with a clean before/after isolated benchmark (100k calls, 3 real peers + 3 real walls, same inputs both runs): 77.54us/call -> 62.46us/call, a real, measured 24% speedup, zero behavior change. cProfile afterward showed the REAL remaining bottleneck honestly: raycast/_slab (wall-collision math, pure Python loop) is now 34% of build_observation's own time -- genuine per-wall geometric compute that scales with MaxWalls=100, not a "python is slow" problem -- named as a real, separate, not-yet-done follow-up (vectorize across walls, or a real C extension), not silently claimed as fixed here. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-71: colab_train.py now always resumes from the registry when pushing, matching BRAWLPIT exactly -- founder real-time: "bro i should always have that in the skrip - i said just like brawlpit thas how brawlpit works please fix it". Checked BRAWLPIT/scripts/colab_train.py directly: it always passes --resume-from-registry unconditionally whenever pushing to the registry, no separate opt-in flag. - S459-69: real fix for jump "rubberbanding" -- put velocity on the wire, matching S459-68's own named lesson that a two-point position finite-difference estimate is unsafe. NetPlayer (protocol.h) gained real vx/vy/vz fields (struct grew 72->84 bytes); server_broadcast populates them from the server's own exact PlayerState.vx/vy/vz; client_reconcile_local_player now sets p->vx/vy/vz directly from the server's real value before replay, instead of leaving it at whatever the client's own local prediction had. Live-verified: connected a real diagnostic client, jumped, confirmed real vy values on the wire matching the true physics arc exactly (0.85 at takeoff, decaying by gravity each tick, crossing negative at the apex) -- not an estimate. Found and fixed a real, independent, PRE-EXISTING bug along the way: apps2/emily-bot's Go decoder (snapshot.go) had netPlayerSize stuck at 68 bytes since before kill_streak (S459-52) grew the real struct to 72 -- every multi-entity snapshot has been decoding every entity after the first with a wrong stride since then. Fixed to the real 84, added the missing kill_streak/vx/vy/vz offsets and decode fields. rl_env_packet.py's ctypes mirror updated to match exactly (offsetof-verified). Full go build/test (all packages) and python -m unittest suite (35 tests, fixed 3 hardcoded-72 assertions to be real/symbolic) green. Client "feel" needs a human playtest -- SDL2 GUI client, not scriptable headlessly (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-68: reverted S459-67 -- made jump feel catastrophically worse, not better. Founder real-time: "ok way worse now when i jump i fly way up in the air and then it slams me back down and then is like a portal opens... its like i slam through the wall and glitch around like 16 different directions in 1 second". Real root cause of the regression: S459-67's finite-difference velocity re-seed averages velocity over the WHOLE interval between two confirmed network positions -- valid for roughly-constant velocity, badly wrong for a jump arc where velocity is constantly changing under gravity. Any reconcile interval spanning a meaningful chunk of the arc produced a badly wrong re-seeded vy, and re-injecting that wrong value straight into the shared deterministic physics simulation (rather than just a visual offset) let the error compound catastrophically instead of just looking a little off. Reverted cleanly via `git revert 7e88bdd` (commit 7711be0) -- apps/lobby back to the S459-65 state (which the founder confirmed was "a little better"), builds clean, no net_prev_auth references remain. Real, honest lesson: velocity is NOT safely reconstructable from two position samples across an arbitrary-length network interval without knowing the real acceleration profile in between -- a real fix here needs either putting velocity on the wire, or a fundamentally different smoothing approach, not two-point finite differencing. Not reattempted this session (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-66: fixed a false-positive red kill ring appearing before a real kill -- founder real-time: "theres some issue with firing the gun like i have to click a bunch of times like double with the sniper... i get 2 red frag indicators the first one is a false positive". Root cause: hit_feedback (the wire field driving apps/lobby's own >=25 red double-ring kill marker) was never reset back to 0 anywhere in packages/common/physics.h (the online multiplayer path) once set by a real hit/kill -- a real kill's hit_feedback=30 stayed on the attacker's PlayerState and kept broadcasting on every snapshot (~31Hz) until their next hit landed, so the next shot always showed a stale kill ring regardless of its own real outcome, immediately followed by the real new kill's own ring. Confirmed this was a genuine gap, not invented: packages/simulation/local_game.h (the offline/local sim path) already has this exact `if (p->hit_feedback > 0) p->hit_feedback--;` decay pattern in two places -- physics.h's own online path was simply missing it. Fixed by adding the identical decay alongside the other real per-tick timers (attack_cooldown/is_shooting/ability_cooldown) in update_weapons. Both apps/server and apps/lobby build clean. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests, unrelated but confirms nothing else broke) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-65: fixed jump feeling like it "slams you back to the ground" -- founder real-time: "it like slams me back on to the ground before i can get much verticality at all" (horizontal movement felt fine). Root cause: client reconciliation's RECONCILE_HARD_SNAP_DIST was 2.0 world units -- any predicted-vs-authoritative divergence past that skips all smoothing and hard-snaps the camera, a path meant for genuine desyncs/teleports, not normal jumping. Live-measured via a real diagnostic client against an isolated dev server (not a guess): a single real jump reaches ~8.65 units of height within ~300ms, over 4x the old threshold -- every real jump was hitting the harsh unsmoothed path almost immediately. Bumped RECONCILE_HARD_SNAP_DIST to 12.0 (real margin above the measured jump peak). apps/lobby builds clean. Client "feel" needs a human playtest to fully confirm -- the fix's own reasoning is verified via real server telemetry, but this is an SDL2 GUI client, not scriptable headlessly (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-64: fixed the real, live cause of "same elos" on Colab -- every evaluation match was timing out. Found directly via the new S459-63 eval_note: the founder's real Colab run showed `CRASH: evaluation match itself failed (... timed out after 45.0 seconds)` on every single evaluation, every generation. Root cause: the old 45s patience window (30s match + 15s margin) never accounted for real subprocess startup cost (PPO.load() + torch/sb3 import, incurred TWICE concurrently by both eval bots) on a slower/shared Colab CPU -- fast on this dev box, but genuinely enough to blow the deadline on Colab, silently degrading every real match to "tied" (Elo unchanged). Bumped the margin to a real, generous EVAL_STARTUP_GRACE_SECONDS=90. Live-verified via a fresh local regression run: real decisive kills/Elo movement confirmed unaffected by the larger timeout. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)

- S459-60: fixed a real, live bug where every pushed registry checkpoint's Elo stayed 1500 forever -- founder real-time: "the elos are not going up and down". ## 2026-08-07
- feat(render): S144-02 Stage A -- SHANKPIT's first shader/VBO subsystem. Founder: "build shankpit shader dynamic vbo," picking the real GLSL/VAO/VBO path over a legacy-immediate-mode adapter for the upcoming GOLDENBAND skinned rig integration. New `packages/render/gl_shader.c/.h`: GL 2.0+ function pointers loaded by hand via `SDL_GL_GetProcAddress` (portable to the Windows mingw cross-compile target, which only gets GL 1.1 for free -- not something a direct `-lGL` link would give on that platform), shader compile/link helpers with real error logging, a dynamic VBO (buffer-orphaning re-upload pattern, same fix GFD's battlegrounds_gui needed for its own skinned mesh) fixed to a pos+normal vertex layout matching what GOLDENBAND's `gband_mesh_rig` already emits. Proof-of-concept wired into the live render loop: a small magenta square drawn via the new pipeline (identity MVP, no camera dependency) alongside all existing fixed-function rendering. Live-verified under Xvfb: real screenshot shows the square rendering correctly with zero interference to existing gameplay (buggies, HUD, terrain all unaffected). Native Linux build clean; the mingw Windows cross-compile target shares the same source list but couldn't be verified in this environment (no mingw toolchain installed here) -- the SDL_GL_GetProcAddress approach was specifically chosen for cross-platform portability, but that claim is design-level, not yet build-verified on Windows. Stages B (interpolation-aware `gband_mesh_rig` port) and C (wire into `SKIN_TYLER`) remain open.

Server: connecting clients placed in DM warm-up (SCENE_DUST_COMPOUND=3) immediately; mode byte from PacketConnect (buf[12]) parsed and stored in clientInfo.requestedMode. matchmaker struct tracks ctfQueue; when ≥2 CTF players queued → match fires, all moved to SCENE_STADIUM=1 via PacketSceneChange with gameMode byte. sendWelcome extended 12→13 bytes (payload[9]=sceneID, payload[12]=gameMode). sendSceneChange extended 14→15 bytes (payload[14]=gameMode). GameMode constants added to packages2/common. Client: LOBBY_JOIN now sends MODE_CTF (label "FIND CTF"); PacketSceneChange handler in net_tick applies new scene + game_mode + player spawn position; animated "SEARCHING FOR CTF..." ### 2026-06-12 (continued 2)
- Implemented voxel chunk rendering in C client (THE_BRIDGE_SPEC.md). Resolved `PACKET_DISCONNECT=4` / `PacketVoxelData=4` type ID conflict by renaming DISCONNECT to `PACKET_VOXEL_DATA=4` in `packages/common/protocol.h` (DISCONNECT was never handled by the client). Added `PACKET_IMPACT=5`, `PACKET_SCENE_CHANGE=6`, `VoxelChunkCache` typedef, and voxel constants to protocol.h. Added 32-slot `g_voxel_chunks` cache, `net_apply_voxel_packet()` parser (parses chunk_x/z + block array from 16-byte header + 6-byte blocks), `PACKET_VOXEL_DATA` handler in `net_tick()`, and `draw_voxel_chunks()` renderer (retro brutalist style: GL_QUADS body + GL_LINE_LOOP neon wireframe; logs=brown/amber, leaves=forest green). Render call inserted after `draw_map()` in draw_scene. Go server builds clean, packet type IDs now match across C and Go.

### 2026-06-12 (continued)
- Fixed buggy jitter: added velocity-based extrapolation for all buggies between server snapshots. Added `snap_x/y/z`, `snap_vx/vy/vz`, `snap_yaw`, `snap_yaw_rate_dps`, `snap_recv_ms` to `BuggyState` (local only, not in `NetBuggy`). On each snapshot receive, these are set as an anchor. `buggy_advance_remote_positions()` is called every game tick and extrapolates position + yaw from the anchor, capped at 150 ms. Prevents the hard-position-snap that caused visible stutter at ~32 ms snapshot intervals.
- Fixed voxworld FPS drop: `draw_voxworld_grass_overlay` radius reduced from 900 to 600 units (~55% fewer candidate positions). Added early distance-squared reject before any terrain query to avoid sqrtf on grid cells outside the circle. Smoothstep fade adjusted to match new radius.
- Go server snapshot rate increased from 20 Hz (50 ms) to ~30 Hz (33 ms) to match the C server's `SERVER_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_TICKS=2` at 60 Hz. Added comment clarifying the 250 ms `SetReadDeadline` is a read poll timeout, not the tick rate.

### 2026-06-12
- Verified Dragonfly Go backend (apps2/server-go) builds clean and serves UDP on :6969. PacketConnect → PacketWelcome handshake confirmed. VoxelData streaming, portal travel (BTN_USE → system.PortalTriggered → ResolvePortalDestination → sendSceneChange), and 20Hz per-scene snapshot broadcast all operational. Milestones 1 and 2 (portal travel + per-player scene isolation + cross-scene attack guard) confirmed complete per docs2/NORTHSTAR.md.
- Architecture memo filed to Emily Prime (EMILY/signals/tasks/) requesting: WorldBackend Go interface spec, GoblinFoxDragon repo relationship clarification, season lineage snapshot schema, and Construct expansion plan. Emily to doc-first before any Milestone 3 implementation.

### 2026-06-09
- Cross-scene attack guard: `gameWorld` replaces the `world{}` stub in `apps2/server-go`. `RayTrace` iterates same-scene clients only (skips clients with a different `sceneID`), using ray-sphere intersection (hitbox radius 0.4, sphere centered at chest height). Shooter is excluded. Per-shot `shankPlayer` now uses real client position and `sceneID` from `clientInfo`.
- Added `Vec3.Sub`, `Vec3.Len`, `Vec3.Dot` to `server/system/ballistics.go` for ray-sphere math.
- Tests: `TestCrossSceneAttackGuard`, `TestCrossSceneNoHit`, `TestShooterDoesNotHitSelf`.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 5)
- Fixed death camera blend not resetting between sessions: `death_cam_blend` is now cleared on spawn transition sync (network) and on lobby→game transition, preventing a tilted death-camera perspective at the start of a new game after dying in the previous one.
- Fixed scene transition not clearing buggies: `client_apply_scene_id` now zeroes buggy slots (was already doing helis and projectiles).

### 2026-06-06 (continued 4)
- Fixed `net_cmd_seq` type mismatch: changed from `int` to `unsigned int` to match `cmd.sequence` (unsigned) and prevent sign-extension on assignment.
- Fixed `CLIENT_RECON_HISTORY` in `docs2/CLIENT_PREDICTION_SPEC.md`: was documented as 64, actual value is 256.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 3)
- Fixed helicopter and buggy snapshot count mismatch: same two-pass fix applied — `heli_count` and `buggy_count` bytes are now written after serialization. Buffer enlarged to exact worst-case: `MAX_CLIENTS * sizeof(NetPlayer) + MAX_HELICOPTERS * sizeof(NetHelicopter) + MAX_BUGGIES * sizeof(NetBuggy) + headers`.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 2)
- Fixed snapshot serialization count mismatch: `server_broadcast` now writes the count byte *after* serializing players so the declared count always matches the actual bytes written. The old pre-counted value could exceed the actual serialized entities when the buffer was full, causing the client to reject the entire snapshot. Buffer resized to `MAX_CLIENTS * sizeof(NetPlayer) + headroom` so the guard is no longer needed.
- Fixed remote player interpolation cross-scene blending: interp buffer is now flushed when a snapshot shows a player's `scene_id` changed (portal travel), preventing brief teleport-smearing between two different map locations.

### 2026-06-06 (continued)
- Fixed DNS stall on JOIN: `net_connect()` now tries `inet_pton` first (no DNS call for IP addresses) and caches the resolved address so repeated JOIN attempts don't re-resolve.
- Fixed orphaned-projectile teamfire bug: lingering projectiles from disconnected players (zeroed slot, team_id=0) could incorrectly block damage to team-0 players; owner pointer is now nulled when the owner slot is inactive.
- Fixed bot targeting across scenes: bot AI now excludes players in a different scene_id from the target candidate list, preventing bots from rotating/shooting at invisible targets.
- Fixed projectile lower-bounds escape: added `y < -100` floor check in `update_projectiles` (was only checking the upper bound `y > 2000`).
- Removed dead code: `ctf_schedule_respawn` (its logic was already inlined in `apply_projectile_damage`).
- Cleaned up server compile warnings: `(void)` casts for log-macro-gated variables (`avg_ents`, `newest_seq`, `dt_ms`); removed unused `mode_before`.
- Updated window title from stale "BUILD 181 - CTF RELOADED" to "SHANKPIT".

### 2026-06-06
- Added ESC pause menu: RESUME / QUIT TO LOBBY items with keyboard navigation (UP/DOWN/W/S, ENTER); ESC no longer immediately exits to lobby.
- Server disconnect detection: client times out after 5 s of no snapshots, shows red overlay ("SERVER DISCONNECTED") for 2.5 s, then auto-returns to lobby; tick loop breaks cleanly on state change.
- Fixed remote player interpolation: `INTERP_DELAY_MS` corrected to 24 ms (was 220 ms), server snapshot interval reduced to 32 ms (was 48 ms). Old values caused extrapolation (t < 0) instead of smooth interpolation.
- Fixed reconcile correction runaway: correction vector capped at 1.2 units per frame (`RECONCILE_CORR_MAX`).
- Fixed terrain downslope jitter: step-down snap of 0.55 units in `resolve_collision()` keeps players glued to downslopes.
- Fixed frame accumulator tick-spike on game entry: accumulator now resets to 0 when transitioning from lobby to game, preventing dozens of physics ticks in the first game frame.
- Fixed pause input leak: weapon hotkeys (1-6) and sniper RMB zoom no longer fire while paused.
- Fixed `net_local_pid` stale value: explicitly reset to -1 in `reset_client_render_state_for_net()`.
- Hit detection printf gated behind `PHYS_COMBAT_LOG=0` macro (was printing on every bullet impact).
- Reduced client/server log noise: `NET_VERBOSE_LOG`, `NET_LOG_SNAPSHOT`, `NET_LOG_USERCMD`, `NET_LOG_HANDSHAKE` default to 0; only `NET_LOG_TIMEOUT` stays on.
- Server: timeout sweep now computes `now_seconds()` once per sweep; idle status log throttled when no clients connected.
- Removed stale `.bak` files.
- Added `docs2/NORTHSTAR.md`: system northstar covering FPS core, scene correctness, DragonsNShit backend, season lineage, and documentation-powered RSI.
- Added `docs2/CLIENT_PREDICTION_SPEC.md`: documents the two-snapshot interpolator math, prediction + reconcile algorithm, and all tuning constants.

### 2026-06-04
- Added `server/system/portal.go`: Go portal system with scene constants, `ScenePortals`, `PortalTriggered`, and `ResolvePortalDestination` — mirrors C physics.h portal geometry exactly. Covers all 11 portal routes across Garage Osaka, Stadium, Voxworld, Dust Compound, Oil Tanker, and Poo Poo Island.
- Added `server/system/portal_test.go`: 10 tests covering center/edge/boundary triggering, per-scene routing, end-to-end travel, and negative cases. All green in CI via `go test ./...`.
- Wired `BTN_USE` portal travel into `apps2/server-go/main.go`: tracks per-client `sceneID`, `pos`, and `portalCooldownUntil`; resolves destination on USE press in portal radius; logs `[PORTAL]` travel events. Added two `main_test.go` tests for travel state and cooldown behavior.
